class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">3</span></span></span></span>; <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>19</mn><mo>≡</mo><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">19 \equiv -1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">19</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">≡</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>. So every order divides 4.</p>
</details>
<p><strong>4.</strong> Use the conjugation rule to decide whether <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>H</mi><mo>=</mo><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mi>e</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mtext> </mtext><mn>2</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>3</mn><mtext> </mtext><mn>4</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">H = \{e, (1 \ 2)(3 \ 4)\}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.08125em;">H</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">{</span><span class="mord mathnormal">e</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mspace"> </span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">3</span><span class="mspace"> </span><span class="mord">4</span><span class="mclose">)}</span></span></span></span> is normal in <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>4</mn></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S_4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.05764em;">S</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3011em;"><span mtight">4</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, and whether <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">V</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.22222em;">V</span></span></span></span> is.</p>
<details>
<summary>Solution</summary>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>H</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">H</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.08125em;">H</span></span></span></span> is not normal: conjugating by <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext> </mtext><mn>3</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(2 \ 3)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mspace"> </span><span class="mord">3</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> gives <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mtext> </mtext><mn>3</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mtext> </mtext><mn>4</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo mathvariant="normal">∉</mo><mi>H</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(1 \ 3)(2 \ 4) \notin H</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mspace"> </span><span class="mord">3</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mspace"> </span><span class="mord">4</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel"><span class="mord"><span class="mrel">∈</span></span><span class="mord vbox"><span class="thinbox"><span class="llap"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="inner"><span class="mord"><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.0556em;"></span></span></span><span class="fix"></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.08125em;">H</span></span></span></span>. <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>V</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">V</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.22222em;">V</span></span></span></span> is normal: it consists of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>e</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">e</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">e</span></span></span></span> and all permutations with cycle type two disjoint transpositions, and conjugation preserves cycle type.</p>
</details>
<p><strong>5.</strong> In <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi mathvariant="double-struck">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mn>18</mn><mi mathvariant="double-struck">Z</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mn>6</mn><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(\mathbb{Z}/18\mathbb{Z})/\langle 6 \rangle</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathbb">Z</span><span class="mord">/18</span><span class="mord mathbb">Z</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span class="mord">6</span><span class="mclose">⟩</span></span></span></span>, find the order of the quotient, the order of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mn>6</mn><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4 + \langle 6 \rangle</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">4</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span class="mord">6</span><span class="mclose">⟩</span></span></span></span>, and whether the quotient is cyclic.</p>
<details>
<summary>Solution</summary>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mn>6</mn><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo><mo>=</mo><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mn>0</mn><mo separator="true">,</mo><mn>6</mn><mo separator="true">,</mo><mn>12</mn><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\langle 6 \rangle = \{0, 6, 12\}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span class="mord">6</span><span class="mclose">⟩</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">{</span><span class="mord">0</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord">6</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord">12</span><span class="mclose">}</span></span></span></span> has order 3, so the quotient has order <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>18</mn><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mn>3</mn><mo>=</mo><mn>6</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">18/3 = 6</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord">18/3</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">6</span></span></span></span>. The order of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo>+</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mn>6</mn><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4 + \langle 6 \rangle</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">4</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span class="mord">6</span><span class="mclose">⟩</span></span></span></span> is the least <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.03148em;">k</span></span></span></span> with <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>4</mn><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mn>0</mn><mo separator="true">,</mo><mn>6</mn><mo separator="true">,</mo><mn>12</mn><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4k \in \{0, 6, 12\}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7335em;vertical-align:-0.0391em;"></span><span class="mord">4</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.03148em;">k</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">∈</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">{</span><span class="mord">0</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord">6</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord">12</span><span class="mclose">}</span></span></span></span> modulo 18: <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>4</mn><mo separator="true">,</mo><mn>8</mn><mo separator="true">,</mo><mn>12</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">4, 8, 12</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8389em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord">4</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord">8</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord">12</span></span></span></span>, so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k = 3</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.03148em;">k</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">3</span></span></span></span>. The coset <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn><mo>+</mo><mo stretchy="false">⟨</mo><mn>6</mn><mo stretchy="false">⟩</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1 + \langle 6 \rangle</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">⟨</span><span xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.03148em;">k</span></span></span></span> with <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi><mo>∈</mo><mo stretchy="false">{</mo><mn>0</mn><mo separator="true">,</mo><mn>6</mn><mo separator="true">,</mo><mn>12</mn><mo stretchy="false">}</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k \in \{0, 6, 12\}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7335em;vertical-align:-0.0391em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.03148em;">k</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">∈</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">{</span><span class="mord">0</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord">6</span><span class="mpunct">,</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord">12</span><span class="mclose">}</span></span></span></span> is 6), so the quotient is cyclic, isomorphic to <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="double-struck">Z</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mn>6</mn><mi mathvariant="double-struck">Z</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbb{Z}/6\mathbb{Z}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathbb">Z</span><span class="mord">/6</span><span class="mord mathbb">Z</span></span></span></span>.</p>
</details>
<p><strong>6.</strong> Prove that <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">∣</mo><mi>G</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mi>Z</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>G</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">∣</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\lvert G/Z(G) \rvert</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">∣</span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.07153em;">Z</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mclose">)∣</span></span></span></span> is never a prime number.</p>
<details>
<summary>Solution</summary>
<p>If <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">∣</mo><mi>G</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mi>Z</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>G</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">∣</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\lvert G/Z(G) \rvert = p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">∣</span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.07153em;">Z</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mclose">)∣</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span> is prime, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mi>Z</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>G</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G/Z(G)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.07153em;">Z</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> is cyclic (Corollary 2.4), so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span></span></span></span> is abelian (Theorem 6.2). Then <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>Z</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>G</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>G</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">Z(G) = G</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.07153em;">Z</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">∣</mo><mi>G</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mi>Z</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>G</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">∣</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\lvert G/Z(G) \rvert = 1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">∣</span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.07153em;">Z</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mclose">)∣</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>, not <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span></span></span></span>. Contradiction.</p>
</details>
<p><strong>7.</strong> Find the commutator subgroup of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math <summary>Solution</summary>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>r</mi><mo separator="true">,</mo><mi>s</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mi>a</mi><mi>b</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">(aN)(bN) = abN</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">a</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.10903em;">N</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal">b</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.10903em;">N</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ab</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.10903em;">N</span></span></span></span>, needs <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>N</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">N</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.10903em;">N</span></span></span></span> normal</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Order in <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mi>N</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G/N</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.10903em;">N</span></span></span></span></td>
<td>least <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>k</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">k</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.03148em;">k</span></span></span></span> with <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>g</mi><mi>k</mi></msup><mo>∈</mo><mi>N</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">g^k \in N</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1.0435em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.03588em;">g</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8491em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight" style="margin-right:0.03148em;">k</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">∈</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.10903em;">N</span></span></span></span></td>

<h1 id="Whats-next"><a href="#Whats-next" class="headerlink" title="What's next"></a>What's next</h1>
<p>Post 03 introduces <strong>homomorphisms</strong>, maps between groups that respect the operation. Their kernels are exactly the normal subgroups, and the <strong>first isomorphism theorem</strong> says <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mi>ker</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi>ϕ</mi><mo>≅</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">im</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mi>ϕ</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">G/\ker\phi \cong \operatorname{im}\phi</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mop">ker</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">≅</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mop"><span class="mord mathrm">im</span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">ϕ</span></span></span></span>, which turns statements such as <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>G</mi><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mi>S</mi><msub><mi>L</mi><mi>n</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>≅</mo><msup><mi mathvariant="double-struck">R</mi><mo>×</mo></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">GL_n(\mathbb{R})/SL_n(\mathbb{R}) \cong \mathbb{R}^\times</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">G</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">L</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span mtight">n</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathbb">R</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.05764em;">S</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">L</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">n</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathbb">R</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">≅</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7713em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathbb">R</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.7713em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mbin mtight">×</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathbb">R</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord mathbb">Z</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">≅</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">1</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> into one-line proofs. <strong>Group actions</strong> give the orbit–stabiliser theorem and the class equation (which supplies the conjugacy class sizes used for <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>A</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">A_5</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">A</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3011em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">5</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> above), and the <strong>Sylow theorems</strong> give the partial converse of Lagrange's theorem that Chapter 3 promised.</p>

<ul>
<li>David S. Dummit and Richard M. Foote, <em>Abstract Algebra</em>, 3rd edition, Wiley, 2004 — Sections 3.1–3.2 (quotient groups, Lagrange's theorem) and 3.4 (composition series, Jordan–Hölder).</li>
<li>Joseph A. Gallian, <em>Contemporary Abstract Algebra</em>, 10th edition, CRC Press, 2021 — Chapters 7 (cosets and Lagrange's theorem) and 9 (normal subgroups and factor groups).</li>
<li>Michael Artin, <em>Algebra</em>, 2nd edition, Pearson, 2011 — Chapter 2, sections on cosets, normal subgroups and quotient groups.</li>
<li>John B. Fraleigh, <em>A First Course in Abstract Algebra</em>, 7th edition, Addison-Wesley, 2003 — Sections 10 (cosets and Lagrange), 14–15 (factor groups, simple groups).</li>
<li>Thomas W.
